### TRADE #59 CLOSED -- stopped out, close-based fill lands BEYOND both the nominal stop and entry, flipping a marginally-positive trigger level into a genuine small loss (trade-critical immediate write)
TIME: 2020-06-04 10:45:00 UTC (T=1591267500, verified via python3; re-fetched via count=3 to
confirm this is a genuine settled bar, not a forming-bar placeholder -- volume 475 is a clean
integer, consistent with real data)
BAR: O 1710.362 / H 1715.781 / L 1709.91 / C 1712.662, volume 475 (sub-threshold, not a
real-volume reversal signal -- this is a genuine but thin-volume close beyond the stop)

Trailed stop was 1711.9 (set 2020-06-03 14:00 UTC). This bar's CLOSE (1712.662) is beyond that
level, triggering the stop per the permanent close-based convention. EXIT_FILL = 1712.662 (the
triggering bar's own close), NOT the nominal 1711.9 level.

RESULT: SHORT, entry 1712.008, exit 1712.662, RESULT_POINTS = -0.654, INITIAL_RISK_POINTS = 14.138,
REALIZED_RESULT_R = -0.046R.

This is a direct, stark real-world demonstration of the CEO's standing correction
(TRAIL_TRIGGER_LEVEL_R vs REALIZED_RESULT_R): at the moment of the trail (2020-06-03 14:00 UTC),
the nominal stop (1711.9) implied a TRAIL_TRIGGER_LEVEL_R of +0.008R (marginally positive, since
1711.9 sat just below entry). The ACTUAL close-based fill (1712.662) landed not just past the
nominal stop but past the entry price itself, flipping the outcome to a small but genuine LOSS
(-0.046R). This is a sharper illustration than trade #58's case (where both figures were positive,
just different magnitudes) -- here the sign itself flips. Exactly the mechanical risk the CEO's
correction was warning about: a trail level is a trigger threshold only, never a guaranteed result,
in either direction.

MFE_POINTS = 22.419 (low 1689.589 @ 2020-06-03 14:30 UTC), MFE_R = +1.586.
DATA-INTEGRITY CORRECTION (disclosed, not silently fixed): earlier batch entries in this session
mislabeled this low as "14:15 UTC" and used a stale MFE figure of 19.926pts/+1.412R -- that number

### MATERIAL -- trade #62 in-position update: wick PIERCES the stop level itself, but the close survives by 0.15pts -- the most dramatic close-based-convention demonstration yet
TIME: 2020-06-08 02:00:00 UTC (T=1591581600, verified via python3)
BAR: O 1686.254 / H 1688.924 / L 1685.918 / C 1688.353, volume 1535 (sub-threshold)

This bar's HIGH (1688.924) actually exceeds the stop (1688.5) by 0.424pts -- the wick genuinely
pierced the stop level. But the bar's CLOSE (1688.353) remains just 0.147pts BELOW the stop. Per
the permanent close-based convention, a stop only triggers on a CLOSE beyond the level, never on a
wick alone -- so this trade survives, by the narrowest margin of the entire apprenticeship to date.
Unrealized: -8.186pts / -0.982R, essentially the full initial risk, the deepest of the trade's
life. Volume (1535) remains sub-threshold.

This is the single clearest, most extreme illustration this apprenticeship has produced of why the
close-based convention matters in practice: a wick-based (intrabar) stop would have exited this
trade at this bar. The close-based convention did not.

DECISION: no action -- stop remains at 1688.5 (never widen). Maximum attention continues; this
trade is now essentially at its full risk and could close on the very next bar.

closed back below, surviving margins as tight as 0.147pts -- before this bar's CLOSE (1688.509)
finally crosses it, by the narrowest possible margin of the entire apprenticeship: just 0.009pts.

REALIZED_RESULT_R = -1.001R -- remarkably close to exactly -1.0R despite the extraordinary drama of
four separate tests and three outright wick-piercings of the stop level. This is a striking
counterpoint to trade #60/#61's demonstrations of meaningful overshoot: here the close-based fill
landed almost exactly at the nominal stop, even though the market repeatedly traded through it
intrabar. A further confirmation that wick behavior and close behavior are genuinely independent
under this convention -- overshoot is not guaranteed just because a level gets tested hard.

### ADMINISTRATIVE -- CEO CORRECTION: Multi-Timeframe Trend Alignment V1 installed, replacing the single-tier WITH_TREND label (trade-critical immediate write, prospective only)
TIME: 2020-06-08 ~12:00 UTC (installed during the CEO's directive, applies FORWARD from this point;
does not rewrite any historical data or P&L)

ISSUE IDENTIFIED (by the CEO, following directly from the 10:45 UTC regime-staleness audit): the
single label "WITH_TREND" was too coarse -- a trade could be formally aligned with H4 while
simultaneously fighting an active H1/M15 bullish recovery structure, exactly the ambiguity the
staleness audit surfaced (the current market: H4 formally BEARISH, but H1 has been in a persistent
reclaim/recovery structure since the 1670.438 low).

METHODOLOGY INSTALLED GOING FORWARD (measurement/entry-discipline only):
- The bare label WITH_TREND may no longer be used alone. Every future directional trade freezes
  H4_DIRECTION_RELATION / H1_DIRECTION_RELATION / M15_DIRECTION_RELATION independently
  (ALIGNED / COUNTER / NEUTRAL / TRANSITIONAL / UNCLEAR), plus MULTITIMEFRAME_ALIGNMENT
  (FULLY_ALIGNED / PARTIALLY_ALIGNED / CONFLICTED / TRANSITIONAL / UNCLEAR).
- H4 = context. H1 = active structural phase. M15 = executable directional structure. A SHORT
  during H4=BEARISH but H1/M15 in active bullish recovery is NOT a normal WITH_TREND SHORT -- it
  must be classified H4_ALIGNED_BUT_LOCAL_COUNTERTREND or TRANSITION_CONFLICT_SHORT.
- FORWARD SHORT RULE: while H1/M15 show an active bullish recovery, do not SHORT merely because H4
  is bearish. Require fresh, genuine local bearish re-alignment (failure of the bullish higher-low
  sequence, break of relevant local support with inability to reclaim it, lower-high formation,
  bearish continuation after a break, real-volume acceptance lower) -- not a checklist, not one or
  two bearish candles inside an intact bullish channel. Until then: SHORT_STATE = NOT_TREND_ALIGNED.
- R08_BULLISH_TRANSITION_WATCH = ACTIVE is now tracked explicitly alongside
  REGIME_STALENESS_WARNING = ACTIVE (both set at the 10:45 UTC audit, reaffirmed here). Formal H4
  regime and executable local structure are treated as separate concepts going forward.
- New trade context contract (frozen before every future trade, then the existing six-field
  contract as before): FORMAL_H4_REGIME, H1_ACTIVE_PHASE, M15_ACTIVE_STRUCTURE,
  H4/H1/M15_DIRECTION_RELATION, MULTITIMEFRAME_ALIGNMENT, REGIME_STALENESS_WARNING,
  TRANSITION_WATCH_STATUS, LOCAL_STRUCTURE_REQUIRED_FOR_ENTRY.
- STRATEGY_EVIDENCE_DENOMINATOR.md will separate FULLY_ALIGNED / PARTIALLY_ALIGNED / CONFLICTED /
  TRANSITIONAL SHORTS and LONGs going forward, rather than pooling them into one WITH_TREND bucket.

GOVERNANCE (explicit, honored): no historical P&L, labels, or trade outcomes rewritten; no
reinterpretation of losses to justify the correction; H4 not forced to BULLISH; no strategy created
from this change; no optimization using future outcomes. This is a prospective entry-discipline and
measurement correction only.

HISTORICAL TRADES #58-#62 AUDIT (per CEO instruction -- original labels NOT rewritten, annotation
only, using ONLY contemporaneously frozen entry-time evidence already in TRADE_EVIDENCE_LOG.md, no
hindsight reconstruction):

All five trades' frozen H1_PHASE/M15_STATE tags (written at entry, before outcome was known)
explicitly described the immediate multi-bar move itself as reinforcing the SHORT direction at
every timeframe captured (rejection/breakdown/resumption/resolution-into-breakdown at H1; 2-bar
real-volume directional confirmation at M15) -- so at the IMMEDIATE/LOCAL structural level captured
by those original tags, all five read as FULLY_ALIGNED. Full detail logged to TRADE_EVIDENCE_LOG.md
per-trade. IMPORTANT CAVEAT (disclosed honestly): the original tags captured only the immediate
multi-bar move, not an independent check for a LARGER-DEGREE H1 channel structure coexisting
alongside each entry (the kind of structure just identified in the current 10:45 UTC audit) --
re-deriving that specific question now would require examining intervening price action with
knowledge of what came after, which is exactly the hindsight risk the CEO's governance rule
prohibits. That broader-channel question is therefore NOT_RECOVERABLE_WITHOUT_HINDSIGHT for all
five trades and is left unanswered rather than guessed.

### ADMINISTRATIVE -- ERRATUM: self-discovered 15-minute timestamp mislabeling across three material writes, root cause and correction (no data/decisions affected)
TIME: discovered and corrected at 2020-06-11 19:45:00 UTC (T=1591904700, verified via python3),
while reconstructing the exact bar sequence before a routine batch write.

Three consecutive material writes above -- "sharp real-volume reversal off the fresh high" (originally
labeled 15:15/15:30 UTC), "intense 4-bar real-volume whipsaw battle" (originally labeled
16:00/16:15/16:30/16:45 UTC), and "strong-first-leg countertrend LONG sequence declined" (originally
labeled 16:45/17:00 UTC) -- each mislabeled their bars 15 minutes EARLY, a consistent one-bar-slot
drift. VERIFIED TRUE TIMES (via each bar's own `time` field, cross-checked python3, and confirmed by
open/close chain continuity -- each bar's open exactly equals the prior bar's close throughout):

- "Sharp reversal" write: labeled 15:15/15:30 -> TRUE 15:30/15:45 UTC
- "4-bar whipsaw battle" write: labeled 16:00/16:15/16:30/16:45 -> TRUE 16:15/16:30/16:45/17:00 UTC
- "Strong-first-leg decline" write: labeled 16:45/17:00 -> TRUE 17:00/17:15 UTC

ROOT CAUSE: after the correctly-labeled 15:15 UTC bar (forward-SHORT-rule write, verified correct),
subsequent bars were narrated by incrementing an assumed mental clock rather than reading each
bar's actual `time` field and verifying via python3 -- a single initial slip then propagated
silently through three writes until this reconstruction caught it via the count=8 re-fetch cross-
check (which returned bar 1591896600 as "17:30" when the prior write had called an adjacent bar
"17:15", exposing the inconsistency).

IMPACT: NONE on trading logic or data integrity. All OHLCV values, volumes, directions, and the
same-direction-pairing / elevated-bar-clearance evaluations in the three affected writes remain
fully accurate -- only the wall-clock labels attached to them were wrong. No trade was taken or
missed because of this error (all three writes correctly declined non-qualifying sequences
regardless of their exact clock label). Not rewriting the original entries -- correcting via this
erratum only, consistent with standing governance (no retroactive rewriting of historical
observations).

Reinforcing the standing rule going forward with zero exceptions: verify every batch's bar
timestamps via python3 before writing, every time, not just at 8-bar boundaries.

### ADMINISTRATIVE -- CEO CORRECTION: remove misleading native Risk/Reward geometry for a no-fixed-target trade, replace with primitives-only overlay (visualization only, no replay advancement, Trade #64 record unchanged)
TIME: installed before revealing the bar after 2020-06-15 07:15 UTC, no bar revealed during this
fix.

Trade #64 never had a fixed primary target (trailing/structural management only, per standing
apprenticeship practice) -- the native short_position object, even with profitLevel left at 0, was
identified as misleading because TradingView's native Risk/Reward position geometry inherently
implies a reward/TP region that was never part of the actual trade plan. Removed the native
position (v8ikf7) and its old companion labels (o94Ncd "LIVE_STOP", g3x5rZ "AI_TRADER_T64"), then
rebuilt truthful visualization using primitives only (horizontal_line + text, no
position/Risk-Reward shape type):

- AI_TRADER_T64_ENTRY: horizontal_line, entity_id b7uxXe, price 1740.496 (verified via
  draw_get_properties)
- AI_TRADER_T64_INITIAL_STOP: horizontal_line, entity_id Z9cuuB, price 1744.918 (verified)
- AI_TRADER_T64_FINAL_TRAIL: horizontal_line, entity_id eMvBoN (reused from the prior live-stop
  line, already correctly at the final trail price), price 1733.254 (verified) -- its old label
  removed and replaced by a properly-named companion text (IYOqhj, "AI_TRADER_T64_FINAL_TRAIL")
- AI_TRADER_T64_LABEL: text, entity_id O2iZQs, exact text "SHORT #64\nNO FIXED TP\nTRAILING
  MANAGEMENT\nRESULT = +1.443R" (verified via draw_get_properties, exact match)
- Companion small labels for the entry and initial-stop lines: cfUfO4 ("AI_TRADER_T64_ENTRY"),
  w65feF ("AI_TRADER_T64_INITIAL_STOP")

Confirmed via draw_list: no short_position/long_position entity remains associated with trade #64
-- only horizontal_line + text primitives.

FORWARD RULE installed: if a future trade has a FIXED_PRIMARY_TARGET, the native
LONG_POSITION/SHORT_POSITION object (with both stopLevel and profitLevel overrides) is allowed. If
FIXED_PRIMARY_TARGET = NONE (trailing/structural management only, as has been true for every trade
this apprenticeship to date), use the CUSTOM_POSITION_OVERLAY primitives-only pattern demonstrated
here instead -- never native Risk/Reward geometry, and never profitLevel=0 as a stand-in for "no
target" (that renders a real, if degenerate, TP region rather than truthfully showing none).

TRADE #64 RECORD: UNCHANGED (entry 1740.496, exit 1734.114, +1.443R -- no change to trade state,
P&L, or management history). REPLAY: NOT advanced during this fix.

### ADMINISTRATIVE -- Trade #65 visualization fix, native short_position tool (user feedback, real-time, not replay-time)

USER FEEDBACK (direct, real-time): the manual entry/stop/live-stop lines + rectangle overlay did
not read as an actual taken position -- just scattered lines and text. Instructed to use
TradingView's native short/long position tool instead.

FIX: removed the manual CUSTOM_POSITION_OVERLAY primitives for Trade #65 (entry line, initial-stop
line, live-stop line, bounding rectangle, and their 3 text labels -- entity_ids 0CU73h, 6Hz7Dq,
tEaIJs, UiVF9f, 1IuotP, nVTUag, z48Btk). Replaced with a native `short_position` shape (entity_id
3DpFo4): entry=1724.903, stopLevel=580 ticks (=5.80pts, matching current LIVE_STOP 1730.7 exactly),
profitLevel minimized to 1 tick with profitBackgroundTransparency=100 and all TP-related infoBlocks
hidden (tpPriceOffset/tpPercentOffset/tpTickOffset/tpAmount/tpPL/riskRewardRatio all visible:false)
-- since Trade #65 has no fixed target, the take-profit portion of the native tool is rendered
visually inert rather than fabricating a target. point2 set far in the future (30 days past entry)
so the box visually spans the whole open trade without needing per-bar recreation. Summary label
(Pcn8kf, "SHORT #65 / NO FIXED TP / TRAILING MANAGEMENT / RESULT = OPEN") kept for trade-ID context
the native tool doesn't provide. Verified via draw_get_properties (stopLevel=580, profitLevel=1
confirmed) and a cropped screenshot showing a clear shaded red band bounded by entry and LIVE_STOP
lines, spanning the full open-trade width.

FORWARD RULE UPDATE (supersedes the prior "CUSTOM_POSITION_OVERLAY primitives only" rule): use the
native short_position/long_position tool for all trades going forward, with the profit side
suppressed (profitLevel=1 tick, profitBackgroundTransparency=100, all tp*/riskRewardRatio infoBlocks
hidden) whenever FIXED_PRIMARY_TARGET=NONE. On trail: remove+recreate the entire short_position
shape (same entry, new stopLevel ticks from the new LIVE_STOP), since stop is a level property on
the whole shape, not a separate line object. On close: keep the shape as-is (final stopLevel =
whatever it was trailed to at close), never delete.

TRADE_65_STATE_CHANGED: NO (visualization only, no change to entry/stop/management logic).

USER DIRECTIVE (direct, real-time, follow-up): the box must not keep extending indefinitely --

immediately prior methodology-change entry. Going forward, the frozen entry-time SL/TP levels are
the real, operative exit condition (per the standing close-based stop-fill convention: exit
triggers only on a bar's CLOSE crossing the level, fill = that bar's own close) -- not merely a
frozen RR reference while a separate trailing stop continues to manage the trade.

FORWARD RULE UPDATE (final form, supersedes all prior Trade #65/general visualization rules):
- Every trade's SL and TP are set at entry and frozen (no further trailing as the exit mechanism).

### BUG FIX (critical, user-caught) -- short_position tick size was wrong by 10x, all Trade #65 boxes this session had incorrect SL/TP

USER FEEDBACK (direct, real-time): pointed out the visible SL/TP on their chart weren't at 1732/
1704 as claimed. Investigation (full-screenshot with price axis, comparing native price labels
against intended levels) revealed the root cause: the short_position tool's stopLevel/profitLevel
tick units are 0.001 price-per-tick, NOT 0.01 as assumed from an unrelated prior memory note ("TICK
=0.01"). Every short_position box created this session (gMO1lc, mELoi2, K6IhSA, 6ESRIc, KXpNGV, and
the earlier suppressed-profit iterations) used stopLevel/profitLevel computed as price_points*100,
which is 10x too small -- e.g. the "734/2042" box intended to be SL=1732.242/TP=1704.484 actually
rendered SL=1725.637/TP=1722.861 (0.734pts/2.042pts from entry, not 7.34pts/20.42pts).

FIX: recreated the frozen SL/TP box (final entity_id niCWb0, superseding KXpNGV) with the corrected
conversion (price_points / 0.001 = price_points*1000): stopLevel=7339 (7.339pts, matches SL
1732.242 exactly), profitLevel=20419 (20.419pts, matches TP 1704.484 exactly). VERIFIED via a
full-screenshot (not just the "chart" region) showing the tool's own native price-axis labels
reading exactly "1,732.242" and "1,704.484" -- unambiguous, not just an API-property check this
time.

TRADE #65 RECORD: entry/SL/TP/result/close-time all UNCHANGED (1724.903 / 1732.242 / 1704.484 /
-1.119R / 2020-06-18 08:45 UTC) -- this was purely a chart-rendering bug, the actual trade
computation in this log and TRADE_EVIDENCE_LOG.md was always correct (computed directly in price
points via python3, never relying on the buggy tick conversion).

FORWARD RULE UPDATE (critical, permanent): for the short_position/long_position tool, ticks =
price_points / 0.001 (i.e. price_points * 1000), NOT price_points * 100. ALWAYS verify a newly
created box via a FULL screenshot (not just "chart" region) showing the tool's own native
price-axis labels matching the intended SL/TP -- draw_get_properties alone only echoes back the
input tick count, it does NOT catch a wrong tick-to-price conversion.

### ADMINISTRATIVE -- CEO DECISION: Structural TP Execution Buffer V1 installed, PROSPECTIVE ONLY (real-time, no replay advancement)

Installed per direct CEO decision (real-time). Does NOT modify Trade #66 or any prior trade --
#66's FROZEN_TP remains 1747.566, unchanged, exactly as originally set.

RULE: the structural target (first realistic pre-entry-visible objective, per existing methodology)
remains the analytical market objective and continues to be identified and frozen exactly as
before. A new EXECUTABLE_TP is additionally frozen at entry, placed 10 project pips (=1.000 price
point; project convention 10 pips = $1.00 XAUUSD movement, consistent with the existing "10
pips=$1.00" convention used elsewhere in this project) BEFORE the structural target, to reduce the
risk of price approaching a structural level, reacting just short of it, and leaving the trade
unresolved:
- SHORT: EXECUTABLE_TP = STRUCTURAL_TARGET + 1.000
- LONG: EXECUTABLE_TP = STRUCTURAL_TARGET - 1.000

The close-based execution convention is UNCHANGED: the trade closes for real on the first bar
whose CLOSE crosses either the INITIAL_STOP or the EXECUTABLE_TP (not the structural target
directly) -- wicks still never trigger, regardless of distance. EXECUTABLE_TP is a hard profit
objective once frozen: it may not be moved farther away after entry, nor widened because price is
approaching it (mirrors the existing "no trail-loosening" discipline already applied to stops).

SL METHODOLOGY UNCHANGED: the buffer is NOT applied symmetrically to SL. SL remains determined
purely by structural invalidation under its existing, unmodified methodology.

REQUIRED FROZEN FIELDS (every trade from now on, in addition to the existing contract):
STRUCTURAL_TARGET, TP_EXECUTION_BUFFER_PIPS (=10), EXECUTABLE_TP, INITIAL_STOP,
PLANNED_RR_TO_EXECUTABLE_TP. Chart Risk/Reward object (native short_position/long_position tool)
displays ENTRY / INITIAL_STOP / EXECUTABLE_TP via stopLevel/profitLevel; the structural target may
additionally be shown as a separate reference line if useful, per standing visualization rules (no
extra text/labels).

EVIDENCE: STRUCTURAL_TARGET_REACHED (YES/NO) and EXECUTABLE_TP_REACHED (YES/NO) will be tracked
prospectively per trade from now on, to allow future measurement of whether the buffer improves
realized outcomes or cuts too much favorable excursion. The buffer size (10 project pips) is NOT
being optimized retrospectively from Trade #66's near-miss -- it is installed as a fixed, prospective
rule and will only be revisited with its own forward evidence, per this project's standing
governance against retrospective optimization.

CONFIRMATION:
TP_EXECUTION_BUFFER_V1 = ACTIVE
BUFFER = 10_PROJECT_PIPS (1.000 price point)
SL_METHOD_CHANGED = NO
TRADE_66_CHANGED = NO

above FROZEN_SL. Entire bar range (even the low, 1779.51) sits above the frozen stop -- no
ambiguity, this is a clean trigger, not a marginal wick call.

**TRADE #66 CLOSED: LOSS.** Exit fill = bar close = 1783.614 (close-based rule). GROSS = entry -
exit = 1766.952 - 1783.614 = -16.662pts. RESULT_R = -16.662 / 11.922 = -1.398R. Duration: entry
2020-06-24 12:45 UTC -> close 2020-06-30 15:00 UTC = 146.25 hours (585 M15 bars). FROZEN_TP
(1747.566) was never approached favorably at any point in the trade's life -- price moved adverse
almost immediately after entry and never meaningfully reversed.

This closes out the near-miss from 14:30 UTC (high 1778.278, 0.596pts from SL; close 1777.897,
0.977pts clear) exactly as the close-based convention requires: that bar's wick did not trigger,
and the trade continued live for two more 15-minute bars before triggering for real, on its own
close, 30 minutes later. Full evidence detail in TRADE_EVIDENCE_LOG.md ("TRADE #66 -- EVIDENCE
CLOSE").
